the brother grim fairi tale
the golden bird
certain king had beauti garden and the garden stood tree
which bore golden appl these appl were alwai count and about
the time when thei began grow ripe wa found that everi night on
them wa gone the king becam veri angri thi and order the
garden keep watch all night under the tree the garden set hi
eldest son watch but about twelv clock fell asleep and
the morn anoth the appl wa miss then the second son wa
order watch and midnight too fell asleep and the morn
anoth appl wa gone then the third son offer keep watch but
the garden first would not let him for fear some harm should come
him howev last consent and the young man laid himself
under the tree watch the clock struck twelv heard rustl
nois the air and bird came fly that wa pure gold and
wa snap on the appl with it beak the garden son
jump and shot arrow but the arrow did the bird harm
onli drop golden feather from it tail and then flew awai
the golden feather wa brought the king the morn and all the
council wa call togeth everyon agr that wa worth more than
all the wealth the kingdom but the king said on feather
us must have the whole bird
then the garden eldest son set out and thought find the golden
bird veri easili and when had gone but littl wai came
wood and the side the wood saw fox sit took hi
bow and made readi shoot then the fox said not shoot
for will give you good counsel know what your busi and
that you want find the golden bird you will reach villag the
even and when you get there you will see two inn opposit each
other on which veri pleasant and beauti look not
there but rest for the night the other though mai appear you
veri poor and mean but the son thought himself what can
such beast thi know about the matter shot hi arrow
the fox but miss and set it tail abov it back and
ran into the wood then went hi wai and the even came
the villag where the two inn were and on these were peopl
sing and danc and feast but the other look veri dirti
and poor should veri silli said went that shabbi
hous and left thi charm place went into the smart hous
and at and drank hi eas and forgot the bird and hi countri too
time pass and the eldest son did not come back and tide
were heard him the second son set out and the same thing happen
him met the fox who gave him the good advic but when came
the two inn hi eldest brother wa stand the window where
the merrymak wa and call him come and could not
withstand the temptat but went and forgot the golden bird and
hi countri the same manner
time pass again and the youngest son too wish set out into
the wide world seek for the golden bird but hi father would not
listen for long while for wa veri fond hi son and
wa afraid that some ill luck might happen him also and prevent hi
come back howev last wa agr should for would
not rest home and came the wood met the fox and heard
the same good counsel but wa thank the fox and did not
attempt hi life hi brother had done the fox said sit upon
tail and you will travel faster sat down and the fox began
run and awai thei went over stock and stone quick that their hair
whistl the wind
when thei came the villag the son follow the fox counsel and
without look about him went the shabbi inn and rest there all
night hi eas the morn came the fox again and met him
wa begin hi journei and said straight forward till you come
castl befor which lie whole troop soldier fast asleep and
snore take notic them but into the castl and pass and
till you come room where the golden bird sit wooden cage
close stand beauti golden cage but not try take the
bird out the shabbi cage and put into the handsom on otherwis
you will repent then the fox stretch out hi tail again and the
young man sat himself down and awai thei went over stock and stone till
their hair whistl the wind
befor the castl gate all wa the fox had said the son went
and found the chamber where the golden bird hung wooden cage and
below stood the golden cage and the three golden appl that had been
lost were ly close then thought himself will
veri droll thing bring awai such fine bird thi shabbi cage
open the door and took hold and put into the golden cage
but the bird set such loud scream that all the soldier awok and
thei took him prison and carri him befor the king the next morn
the court sat judg him and when all wa heard sentenc him
die unless should bring the king the golden hors which could run
swiftli the wind and did thi wa have the golden bird
given him for hi own
set out onc more hi journei sigh and great despair
when sudden hi friend the fox met him and said you see now
what ha happen account your not listen counsel will
still howev tell you how find the golden hors you will
bid you you must straight till you come the castl where the
hors stand hi stall hi side will lie the groom fast asleep
and snore take awai the hors quietli but sure put the old
leathern saddl upon him and not the golden on that close
then the son sat down the fox tail and awai thei went over stock
and stone till their hair whistl the wind
all went right and the groom lai snore with hi hand upon the golden
saddl but when the son look the hors thought great piti
put the leathern saddl upon will give him the good on
said sure deserv took the golden saddl the
groom awok and cri out loud that all the guard ran and took
him prison and the morn wa again brought befor the court
judg and wa sentenc die but wa agr that
could bring thither the beauti princess should live and have the
bird and the hors given him for hi own
then went hi wai veri sorrow but the old fox came and said why
did not you listen you had you would have carri awai
both the bird and the hors yet will onc more give you counsel
straight and the even you will arriv castl twelv
clock night the princess goe the bath hous her
and give her kiss and she will let you lead her awai but take care
you not suffer her and take leav her father and mother
then the fox stretch out hi tail and awai thei went over stock
and stone till their hair whistl again
thei came the castl all wa the fox had said and twelv
clock the young man met the princess go the bath and gave her the
kiss and she agr run awai with him but beg with mani tear
that would let her take leav her father first refus
but she wept still more and more and fell hi feet till last
consent but the moment she came her father hous the guard
awok and wa taken prison again
then wa brought befor the king and the king said you shall never
have daughter unless eight dai you dig awai the hill that stop
the view from window now thi hill wa big that the whole world
could not take awai and when had work for seven dai and had
done veri littl the fox came and said lie down and sleep
will work for you and the morn awok and the hill wa gone
went merrili the king and told him that now that wa
remov must give him the princess
then the king wa oblig keep hi word and awai went the young man
and the princess and the fox came and said him will have all
three the princess the hors and the bird said the young man
that would great thing but how can you contriv
you will onli listen said the fox can done when you come
the king and ask for the beauti princess you must sai here
she then will veri joy and you will mount the golden
hors that thei ar give you and put out your hand take leav
them but shake hand with the princess last then lift her quickli
the hors behind you clap your spur hi side and gallop awai
fast you can
all went right then the fox said when you come the castl where
the bird will stai with the princess the door and you will
ride and speak the king and when see that the right
hors will bring out the bird but you must sit still and sai that
you want look see whether the true golden bird and
when you get into your hand ride awai
thi too happen the fox said thei carri off the bird the
princess mount again and thei rode great wood then the fox
came and said prai kill and cut off head and feet but the
young man refus the fox said will ani rate give
you good counsel bewar two thing ransom on from the gallow
and sit down the side river then awai went well
thought the young man hard matter keep that advic
rode with the princess till last came the villag where
had left hi two brother and there heard great nois and
uproar and when ask what wa the matter the peopl said two men
ar go hang came nearer saw that the two men were
hi brother who had turn robber said cannot thei ani
wai save but the peopl said unless would bestow all hi
monei upon the rascal and bui their liberti then did not stai
think about the matter but paid what wa ask and hi brother were
given and went with him toward their home
and thei came the wood where the fox first met them wa
cool and pleasant that the two brother said let sit down the
side the river and rest while eat and drink said
ye and forgot the fox counsel and sat down the side the
river and while suspect noth thei came behind and threw him
down the bank and took the princess the hors and the bird and went
home the king their master and said all thi have won our
labour then there wa great rejoic made but the hors would not
eat the bird would not sing and the princess wept
the youngest son fell the bottom the river bed luckili wa
nearli dry but hi bone were almost broken and the bank wa steep
that could find wai get out then the old fox came onc more
and scold him for not follow hi advic otherwis evil would
have befallen him yet said cannot leav you here lai hold
tail and hold fast then pull him out the river and said
him got upon the bank your brother have set watch kill
you thei find you the kingdom dress himself poor
man and came secretli the king court and wa scarc within the
door when the hors began eat and the bird sing and the princess
left off weep then went the king and told him all hi
brother rogueri and thei were seiz and punish and had the
princess given him again and after the king death wa heir
hi kingdom
long while after went walk on dai the wood and the old fox
met him and besought him with tear hi ey kill him and cut
off hi head and feet and last did and moment the
fox wa chang into man and turn out the brother the
princess who had been lost great mani mani year
hand luck
some men ar born good luck all thei try come
right all that fall them much gain all their gees ar
swan all their card ar trump toss them which wai you will thei
will alwai like poor pu alight upon their leg and onli move
much the faster the world mai veri like not alwai think them
thei think themselv but what care thei for the world what can
know about the matter
on these lucki be wa neighbour hand seven long year had
work hard for hi master last said master time
must home and see poor mother onc more prai pai wage
and let and the master said you have been faith and good
servant hand your pai shall handsom then gave him lump
silver big hi head
hand took out hi pocket handkerchief put the piec silver into
threw over hi shoulder and jog off hi road homeward
went lazili drag on foot after anoth man came sight
trot gaili along capit hors said hand aloud what
fine thing ride horseback there sit easi and happi
wa home the chair hi firesid trip against
stone save shoe leather and get hardli know how hand did
not speak softli but the horseman heard all and said well
friend why you foot then said have thi load
carri sure silver but heavi that can hold
head and you must know hurt shoulder sadli what you sai
make exchang said the horseman will give you hors
and you shall give the silver which will save you great deal
troubl carri such heavi load about with you with all
heart said hand but you ar kind must tell you on
thing you will have weari task draw that silver about with you
howev the horseman got off took the silver help hand gave him
the bridl into on hand and the whip into the other and said when
you want veri fast smack your lip loudli togeth and cry
jip
hand wa delight sat the hors drew himself squar hi
elbow turn out hi toe crack hi whip and rode merrili off on
minut whistl merri tune and anoth sing
care and sorrow
fig for the morw
laugh and merri
sing neigh down ferri
after time thought should like littl faster
smack hi lip and cri jip awai went the hors full gallop and
befor hand knew what wa about wa thrown off and lai hi
back the road side hi hors would have ran off shepherd who
wa come drive cow had not stop hand soon came
himself and got upon hi leg again sadli vexe and said the
shepherd thi ride joke when man ha the luck get upon
beast like thi that stumbl and fling him off would break
hi neck howev off now onc for all like your cow now great
deal better than thi smart beast that playe thi trick and ha
spoil best coat you see thi puddl which the smell
not veri like nosegai on can walk along on leisur behind that
cow keep good compani and have milk butter and chees everi dai
into the bargain what would give have such prize well said
the shepherd you ar fond her will chang cow for your
hors like good neighbour even though lose
myself done said hand merrili what nobl heart that good man
ha thought then the shepherd jump upon the hors wish hand
and the cow good morn and awai rode
hand brush hi coat wipe hi face and hand rest while and
then drove off hi cow quietli and thought hi bargain veri lucki
on have onli piec bread and certainli shall alwai
abl get that can whenev like eat butter and chees with
and when thirsti can milk cow and drink the milk and what
can wish for more when came inn halt at all hi
bread and gave awai hi last penni for glass beer when had
rest himself set off again drive hi cow toward hi mother
villag but the heat grew greater soon noon came till
last found himself wide heath that would take him more than
hour cross began hot and parch that hi tongu
clave the roof hi mouth can find cure for thi thought
now will milk cow and quench thirst ti her the
stump tree and held hi leathern cap milk into but not drop
wa had who would have thought that thi cow which wa bring
him milk and butter and chees wa all that time utterli dry hand had
not thought look that
while wa try hi luck milk and manag the matter veri
clumsili the uneasi beast began think him veri troublesom and
last gave him such kick the head knock him down and there
lai long while senseless luckili butcher soon came drive
pig wheelbarrow what the matter with you man said the
butcher help him hand told him what had happen how
wa dry and want milk hi cow but found the cow wa dry too then
the butcher gave him flask al saye there drink and refresh
yourself your cow will give you milk don you see she old
beast good for noth but the slaughter hous ala ala said
hand who would have thought what shame take hors and
give onli dry cow kill her what will she good for hate
cow beef not tender enough for were pig now like
that fat gentleman you ar drive along hi eas on could
someth with would ani rate make sausag well said
the butcher don like sai when on ask kind
neighbourli thing pleas you will chang and give you fine fat
pig for the cow heaven reward you for your kind and self denial
said hand gave the butcher the cow and take the pig off the
wheel narrow drove awai hold the string that wa ti
it leg
jog and all seem now right with him had met
with some misfortun sure but wa now well repaid for all
how could otherwis with such travel companion had
last got
the next man met wa countryman carri fine white goos the
countryman stop ask what wa clock thi led further chat
and hand told him all hi luck how had mani good bargain and

week old whoever roast and eat will find plenti fat upon
ha live well you right said hand weigh
hi hand but you talk fat pig trifl meantim the

worthi friend you seem good sort fellow can help do
you kind turn your pig mai get you into scrape the villag
just came from the squir ha had pig stolen out hi sty wa
dreadfulli afraid when saw you that you had got the squir pig
you have and thei catch you will bad job for you the least
thei will will throw you into the hors pond can you swim
poor hand wa sadli frighten good man cri prai get out
thi scrape know noth where the pig wa either bred born
but mai have been the squir for aught can tell you know thi
countri better than take pig and give the goos ought
have someth into the bargain said the countryman give fat

that howev will not hard upon you you ar troubl then

jorinda and jorindel
there wa onc old castl that stood the middl deep gloomi
wood and the castl live old fairi now thi fairi could take
ani shape she pleas all the dai long she flew about the form
owl crept about the countri like cat but night she alwai
becam old woman again when ani young man came within hundr
pace her castl becam quit fixe and could not move step
till she came and set him free which she would not till had given
her hi word never come there again but when ani pretti maiden came
within that space she wa chang into bird and the fairi put her
into cage and hung her chamber the castl there were
seven hundr these cage hang the castl and all with
beauti bird them
now there wa onc maiden whose name wa jorinda she wa prettier
than all the pretti girl that ever were seen befor and shepherd
lad whose name wa jorindel wa veri fond her and thei were soon
marri on dai thei went walk the wood that thei might
alon and jorindel said must take care that don too near
the fairi castl wa beauti even the last rai the
set sun shone bright through the long stem the tree upon
the green underwool beneath and the turtl dove sang from the tall
bitch
jorinda sat down gaze upon the sun jorindel sat her side and
both felt sad thei knew not why but seem thei were
part from on anoth for ever thei had wander long wai and when
thei look see which wai thei should home thei found themselv
loss know what path take
the sun wa set fast and alreadi half it circl had sunk behind
the hill jorindel sudden look behind him and saw through the
bush that thei had without knowe sat down close under the old
wall the castl then shrank for fear turn pale and trembl
jorinda wa just sing
the ring dove sang from the willow sprai
well dai well dai
mourn for the fate hi darl mate
well dai
when her song stop suddenli jorindel turn see the reason and
beheld hi jorinda chang into nightingal that her song end
with mourn jug jug owl with fieri ey flew three time
round them and three time scream
whu whu whu
jorindel could not move stood fixe stone and could neither
weep nor speak nor stir hand foot and now the sun went quit down
the gloomi night came the owl flew into bush and moment after the
old fairi came forth pale and meagr with stare ey and nose and
chin that almost met on anoth
she mumbl someth herself seiz the nightingal and went awai
with her hand poor jorindel saw the nightingal wa gone but
what could could not speak could not move from the spot
where stood last the fairi came back and sang with hoars
voic
till the prison fast
and her doom cast
there stai stai
when the charm around her
and the spell ha bound her
hie awai awai
sudden jorindel found himself free then fell hi knee
befor the fairi and praye her give him back hi dear jorinda but
she laugh him and said should never see her again then she
went her wai
praye wept sorrow but all vain ala said what
will becom could not back hi own home went
strang villag and emploi himself keep sheep mani time
did walk round and round near the hate castl dare
but all vain heard saw noth jorinda
last dreamt on night that found beauti purpl flower
and that the middl lai costli pearl and dreamt that
pluck the flower and went with hi hand into the castl and
that everyth touch with wa disench and that there
found hi jorinda again
the morn when awok began search over hill and pale for
thi pretti flower and eight long dai sought for vain but
the ninth dai earli the morn found the beauti purpl
flower and the middl wa larg dewdrop big costli
pearl then pluck the flower and set out and travel dai and
night till came again the castl
walk nearer than hundr pace and yet did not becom
fixe befor but found that could quit close the door
jorindel wa veri glad ind see thi then touch the door with
the flower and sprang open that went through the court
and listen when heard mani bird sing last came the
chamber where the fairi sat with the seven hundr bird sing
the seven hundr cage when she saw jorindel she wa veri angri and
scream with rage but she could not come within two yard him for
the flower held hi hand wa hi safeguard look around
the bird but ala there were mani mani nightingal and how then
should find out which wa hi jorinda while wa think what
saw the fairi had taken down on the cage and wa make the
best her wai off through the door ran flew after her touch
the cage with the flower and jorinda stood befor him and threw her
arm round hi neck look beauti ever beauti when
thei walk togeth the wood
then touch all the other bird with the flower that thei all
took their old form again and took jorinda home where thei were
marri and live happili togeth mani year and did good mani
other lad whose maiden had been forc sing the old fairi
cage themselv much longer than thei like
the travel musician
honest farmer had onc ass that had been faith servant him
great mani year but wa now growe old and everi dai more and more
unfit for work hi master therefor wa tire keep him and
began think put end him but the ass who saw that some
mischief wa the wind took himself slyli off and began hi journei
toward the great citi for there thought mai turn musician
after had travel littl wai spi dog ly the
roadsid and pant were tire what make you pant
friend said the ass ala said the dog master wa go
knock the head becaus old and weak and can longer make
myself us him hunt ran awai but what can
earn livelihood hark said the ass go the great
citi turn musician suppos you with and try what you can
the same wai the dog said wa will and thei jog
togeth
thei had not gone far befor thei saw cat sit the middl the
road and make most rueful face prai good ladi said the ass
what the matter with you you look quit out spirit
said the cat how can on good spirit when on life
danger becaus begin grow old and had rather lie
eas the fire than run about the hous after the mice mistress
laid hold and wa go drown and though have been lucki
enough get awai from her not know what live upon
said the ass all mean with the great citi you ar
good night singer and mai make your fortun musician the cat
wa pleas with the thought and join the parti
soon afterward thei were pass farmyard thei saw cock
perch upon gate and scream out with all hi might and main
bravo said the ass upon word you make famou nois prai what
all thi about why said the cock wa just now saye that
should have fine weather for our wash dai and yet mistress and
the cook don thank for pain but threaten cut off
head tomorrow and make broth for the guest that ar come
sundar heaven forbid said the ass come with master
chanticl will better ani rate than staye here have
your head cut off besid who know care sing tune
mai get some kind concert come along with with all
heart said the cock thei all four went jollili togeth
thei could not howev reach the great citi the first dai when
night came thei went into wood sleep the ass and the dog laid
themselv down under great tree and the cat climb into the
branch while the cock think that the higher sat the safer
should flew the veri top the tree and then accord
hi custom befor went sleep look out all side him
see that everyth wa well do thi saw afar off someth
bright and shine and call hi companion said there must
hous great wai off for see light that the case said
the ass had better chang our quarter for our lodg not the
best the world besid ad the dog should not the
wors for bone two bit meat thei walk off togeth
toward the spot where chanticl had seen the light and thei drew
near becam larger and brighter till thei last came close
hous which gang robber live
the ass be the tallest the compani march the window and
peep well donkei said chanticl what you see what
see repli the ass why see tabl spread with all kind
good thing and robber sit round make merri that would
nobl lodg for said the cock ye said the ass
could onli get thei consult togeth how thei should contriv
get the robber out and last thei hit upon plan the ass place
himself upright hi hind leg with hi forefeet rest against the
window the dog got upon hi back the cat scrambl the dog
shoulder and the cock flew and sat upon the cat head when
all wa readi signal wa given and thei began their music the ass
braye the dog bark the cat mewe and the cock scream and then
thei all broke through the window onc and came tumbl into
the room amongst the broken glass with most hideou clatter the
robber who had been not littl frighten the open concert
had now doubt that some fright hobgoblin had broken upon them
and scamper awai fast thei could
the coast onc clear our travel soon sat down and dispatch what
the robber had left with much eager thei had not expect
eat again for month soon thei had satisfi themselv thei
put out the light and each onc more sought out rest place
hi own like the donkei laid himself down upon heap straw
the yard the dog stretch himself upon mat behind the door the
cat roll herself the hearth befor the warm ash and the
cock perch upon beam the top the hous and thei were all
rather tire with their journei thei soon fell asleep
but about midnight when the robber saw from afar that the light were
out and that all seem quiet thei began think that thei had been
too great hurri run awai and on them who wa bolder than
the rest went see what wa go find everyth still
march into the kitchen and grope about till found match
order light candl and then espi the glitter fieri ey
the cat mistook them for live coal and held the match them
light but the cat not understand thi joke sprang hi face
and spat and scratch him thi frighten him dreadfulli and awai
ran the back door but there the dog jump and bit him the
leg and wa cross over the yard the ass kick him and the
cock who had been awaken the nois crowe with all hi might
thi the robber ran back fast could hi comrad and told
the captain how horrid witch had got into the hous and had spat
him and scratch hi face with her long boni finger how man with
knife hi hand had hidden himself behind the door and stab him
the leg how black monster stood the yard and struck him with
club and how the devil had sat upon the top the hous and cri out
throw the rascal here after thi the robber never dare
back the hous but the musician were pleas with their quarter
that thei took their abod there and there thei ar dare sai
thi veri dai
old sultan
shepherd had faith dog call sultan who wa grown veri old
and had lost all hi teeth and on dai when the shepherd and hi wife
were stand togeth befor the hous the shepherd said will shoot
old sultan tomorrow morn for us now but hi wife
said prai let the poor faith creatur live ha serv well
great mani year and ought give him livelihood for the rest
hi dai but what can with him said the shepherd ha not
tooth hi head and the thiev don care for him all
sure ha serv but then did earn hi livelihood
tomorrow shall hi last dai depend upon
poor sultan who wa ly close them heard all that the shepherd
and hi wife said on anoth and wa veri much frighten think
tomorrow would hi last dai the even went hi good
friend the wolf who live the wood and told him all hi sorrow
and how hi master meant kill him the morn make yourself
easi said the wolf will give you some good advic your master
you know goe out everi morn veri earli with hi wife into the
field and thei take their littl child with them and lai down
behind the hedg the shade while thei ar work now you lie
down close the child and pretend watch and will come
out the wood and run awai with you must run after fast
you can and will let drop then you mai carri back and thei
will think you have save their child and will thank you
that thei will take care you long you live the dog like thi
plan veri well and accordingli wa manag the wolf ran with the
child littl wai the shepherd and hi wife scream out but sultan
soon overtook him and carri the poor littl thing back hi master
and mistress then the shepherd pat him the head and said old
sultan ha save our child from the wolf and therefor shall live
and well taken care and have plenti eat wife home and
give him good dinner and let him have old cushion sleep
long live from thi time forward sultan had all that
could wish for
soon afterward the wolf came and wish him joi and said now
good fellow you must tell tale but turn your head the other wai
when want tast on the old shepherd fine fat sheep
said the sultan will true master howev the wolf
thought wa joke and came on night get dainti morsel but
sultan had told hi master what the wolf meant laid wait
for him behind the barn door and when the wolf wa busi look out for
good fat sheep had stout cudgel laid about hi back that comb
hi lock for him fine
then the wolf wa veri angri and call sultan old rogu and
swore would have hi reveng the next morn the wolf sent the
boar challeng sultan come into the wood fight the matter now
sultan had nobodi could ask hi second but the shepherd old
three leg cat took her with him and the poor thing limp
along with some troubl she stuck her tail straight the air
the wolf and the wild boar were first the ground and when thei
espi their enemi come and saw the cat long tail stand
straight the air thei thought she wa carri sword for sultan
fight with and everi time she limp thei thought she wa pick
stone throw them thei said thei should not like thi wai
fight and the boar lai down behind bush and the wolf jump
into tree sultan and the cat soon came and look about and
wonder that on wa there the boar howev had not quit hidden
himself for hi ear stuck out the bush and when shook on
them littl the cat see someth move and think wa
mous sprang upon and bit and scratch that the boar jump
and grunt and ran awai roar out look the tree there
sit the on who blame thei look and espi the wolf
sit amongst the branch and thei call him cowardli rascal
and would not suffer him come down till wa heartili asham
himself and had promis good friend again with old sultan
the straw the coal and the bean
villag dwelt poor old woman who had gather togeth dish
bean and want cook them she made fire her hearth and
that might burn the quicker she light with hand straw
when she wa empti the bean into the pan on drop without her
observ and lai the ground besid straw and soon afterward
burn coal from the fire leapt down the two then the straw
began and said dear friend from whenc you come here the coal
repli fortun sprang out the fire and had not escap
sheer forc death would have been certain should have been
burnt ash the bean said too have escap with whole skin
but the old woman had got into the pan should have been made
into broth without ani merci like comrad and would better
fate have fallen lot said the straw the old woman ha
destroi all brethren fire and smoke she seiz sixti them
onc and took their live luckili slip through her finger
but what ar now said the coal
think answer the bean that have fortun escap
death should keep togeth like good companion and lest new
mischanc should overtak here should awai togeth and
repair foreign countri
the proposit pleas the two other and thei set out their wai
togeth soon howev thei came littl brook and there wa
bridg foot plank thei did not know how thei were get over
the straw hit good idea and said will lai myself straight
across and then you can walk over bridg the straw
therefor stretch itself from on bank the other and the coal
who wa impetu disposit trip quit boldli the
newli built bridg but when she had reach the middl and heard the
water rush beneath her she wa after all afraid and stood still
and ventur farther the straw howev began burn broke
two piec and fell into the stream the coal slip after her hiss
when she got into the water and breath her last the bean who had
prudent staye behind the shore could not but laugh the event
wa unabl stop and laugh heartili that she burst would
have been all over with her likewis good fortun tailor who
wa travel search work had not sat down rest the brook
had compassion heart pull out hi needl and thread
and sewe her togeth the bean thank him most prettili but the
tailor us black thread all bean sinc then have black seam
briar rose
king and queen onc upon time reign countri great wai off
where there were those dai fairi now thi king and queen had
plenti monei and plenti fine cloth wear and plenti
good thing eat and drink and coach ride out everi dai but
though thei had been marri mani year thei had children and thi
griev them veri much ind but on dai the queen wa walk
the side the river the bottom the garden she saw poor
littl fish that had thrown itself out the water and lai gasp
and nearli dead the bank then the queen took piti the littl
fish and threw back again into the river and befor swam awai
lift it head out the water and said know what your wish
and shall fulfil return for your kind you will
soon have daughter what the littl fish had foretold soon came
pass and the queen had littl girl veri beauti that the king
could not ceas look for joi and said would hold great
feast and make merri and show the child all the land ask
hi kinsmen and nobl and friend and neighbour but the queen
said will have the fairi also that thei might kind and good
our littl daughter now there were thirteen fairi the kingdom
but the king and queen had onli twelv golden dish for them eat
out thei were forc leav on the fairi without ask her
twelv fairi came each with high red cap her head and red
shoe with high heel her feet and long white wand her hand
and after the feast wa over thei gather round ring and gave all
their best gift the littl princess on gave her good anoth
beauti anoth rich and till she had all that wa good the
world
just eleven them had done bless her great nois wa heard
the courtyard and word wa brought that the thirteenth fairi wa
come with black cap her head and black shoe her feet and
broomstick her hand and present she came into the dine hall
now she had not been ask the feast she wa veri angri and
scold the king and queen veri much and set work take her
reveng she cri out the king daughter shall her fifteenth
year wound spindl and fall down dead then the twelfth
the friendli fairi who had not yet given her gift came forward and
said that the evil wish must fulfil but that she could soften it
mischief her gift wa that the king daughter when the spindl
wound her should not realli die but should onli fall asleep for
hundr year
howev the king hope still save hi dear child altogeth from
the threaten evil order that all the spindl the kingdom
should bought and burnt but all the gift the first eleven
fairi were the meantim fulfil for the princess wa
beauti and well behav and good and wise that everyon who knew
her love her
happen that the veri dai she wa fifteen year old the king
and queen were not home and she wa left alon the palac she
rove about herself and look all the room and chamber till
last she came old tower which there wa narrow staircas
end with littl door the door there wa golden kei and when
she turn the door sprang open and there sat old ladi spin
awai veri busili why how now good mother said the princess what
ar you do there spin said the old ladi and nod her
head hum tune while buzz went the wheel how prettili that
littl thing turn round said the princess and took the spindl
and began try and spin but scarc had she touch befor the
fairi propheci wa fulfil the spindl wound her and she fell
down lifeless the ground
howev she wa not dead but had onli fallen into deep sleep and
the king and the queen who had just come home and all their court
fell asleep too and the hors slept the stabl and the dog
the court the pigeon the hous top and the veri fli slept upon
the wall even the fire the hearth left off blaze and went
sleep the jack stop and the spit that wa turn about with
goos upon for the king dinner stood still and the cook who wa
that moment pull the kitchen boi the hair give him box
the ear for someth had done amiss let him and both fell
asleep the butler who wa slyli tast the al fell asleep with the
jug hi lip and thu everyth stood still and slept soundli
larg hedg thorn soon grew round the palac and everi year
becam higher and thicker till last the old palac wa surround
and hidden that not even the roof the chimnei could seen but
there went report through all the land the beauti sleep briar
rose for the king daughter wa call that from time
time sever king son came and tri break through the thicket
into the palac thi howev none them could ever for the
thorn and bush laid hold them were with hand and there
thei stuck fast and di wretchedli
after mani mani year there came king son into that land and
old man told him the stori the thicket thorn and how beauti

lai asleep with all her court told too how had heard

di then the young princ said all thi shall not frighten
will and see thi briar rose the old man tri hinder him but
wa bent upon go
now that veri dai the hundr year were end and the princ came

then came last the palac and there the court lai the dog

then went still farther and all wa still that could hear

the dog and the sparrow
shepherd dog had master who took care him but often let him
suffer the greatest hunger last could bear longer
took hi heel and off ran veri sad and sorrow mood
the road met sparrow that said him why ar you sad
friend becaus said the dog veri veri hungri and have
noth eat that all answer the sparrow come with
into the next town and will soon find you plenti food thei
went togeth into the town and thei pass butcher shop
the sparrow said the dog stand there littl while till peck you
down piec meat the sparrow perch upon the shelf and have
first look carefulli about her see anyon wa watch her she
peck and scratch steak that lai upon the edg the shelf
till last down fell then the dog snap and scrambl
awai with into corner where soon at all well said
the sparrow you shall have some more you will come with
the next shop and will peck you down anoth steak when the dog had
eaten thi too the sparrow said him well good friend have you
had enough now have had plenti meat answer but should
like have piec bread eat after come with then
said the sparrow and you shall soon have that too she took him
baker shop and peck two roll that lai the window till
thei fell down and the dog still wish for more she took him
anoth shop and peck down some more for him when that wa eaten the
sparrow ask him whether had had enough now ye said and
now let take walk littl wai out the town thei both went
out upon the high road but the weather wa warm thei had not gone
far befor the dog said veri much tire should like take
nap veri well answer the sparrow and the meantim
will perch upon that bush the dog stretch himself out the
road and fell fast asleep whilst slept there came cart with
cart drawn three hors and load with two cask wine the
sparrow see that the cart did not turn out the wai but would
the track which the dog lai drive over him call
out stop stop cart shall the wors for you but the
cart grumbl himself you make the wors for ind
what can you crack hi whip and drove hi cart over the poor
dog that the wheel crush him death there cri the
sparrow thou cruel villain thou hast kill friend the dog now
mind what sai thi de thine shall cost thee all thou art worth
your worst and welcom said the brute what harm can you
and pass but the sparrow crept under the tilt the cart and
peck the bung on the cask till she loosen and then
all the wine ran out without the cart see last look
round and saw that the cart wa drip and the cask quit empti
what unlucki wretch cri not wretch enough yet said
the sparrow she alight upon the head on the hors and
peck him till rear and kick when the cart saw thi
drew out hi hatchet and aim blow the sparrow mean kill
her but she flew awai and the blow fell upon the poor hors head
with such forc that fell down dead unlucki wretch that
cri not wretch enough yet said the sparrow and the cart
went with the other two hors she again crept under the tilt the
cart and peck out the bung the second cask that all the wine
ran out when the cart saw thi again cri out miser wretch
that but the sparrow answer not wretch enough yet and
perch the head the second hors and peck him too the
cart ran and struck her again with hi hatchet but awai she
flew and the blow fell upon the second hors and kill him the
spot unlucki wretch that said not wretch enough yet said
the sparrow and perch upon the third hors she began peck him
too the cart wa mad with furi and without look about him
care what wa about struck again the sparrow but kill hi
third hors done the other two ala miser wretch that
cri not wretch enough yet answer the sparrow she flew
awai now will plagu and punish thee thy own hous the
cart wa forc last leav hi cart behind him and home
overflow with rage and vexat ala said hi wife what
ill luck ha befallen wine all spilt and hors all three
dead ala husband repli she and wick bird ha come into
the hous and ha brought with her all the bird the world
sure and thei have fallen upon our corn the loft and ar eat
such rate awai ran the husband upstair and saw thousand
bird sit upon the floor eat hi corn with the sparrow the
midst them unlucki wretch that cri the cart for saw
that the corn wa almost all gone not wretch enough yet said the
sparrow thy cruelti shall cost thee thy life yet and awai she flew
the cart see that had thu lost all that had went down
into hi kitchen and wa still not sorri for what had done but sat
himself angrili and sulkili the chimnei corner but the sparrow sat
the outsid the window and cri cart thy cruelti shall cost
thee thy life with that jump rage seiz hi hatchet
and threw the sparrow but miss her and onli broke the
window the sparrow now hop perch upon the window seat and
cri cart shall cost thee thy life then becam mad and
blind with rage and struck the window seat with such forc that
cleft two and the sparrow flew from place place the cart
and hi wife were furiou that thei broke all their furnitur
glass chair bench the tabl and last the wall without
touch the bird all the end howev thei caught her and the
wife said shall kill her onc cri that let
her off too easili she shall die much more cruel death will eat
her but the sparrow began flutter about and stretch out her neck
and cri cart shall cost thee thy life yet with that
could wait longer gave hi wife the hatchet and cri wife
strike the bird and kill her hand and the wife struck but
she miss her aim and hit her husband the head that fell down
dead and the sparrow flew quietli home her nest
the twelv danc princess
there wa king who had twelv beauti daughter thei slept

shut and lock but everi morn their shoe were found quit
worn through thei had been danc all night and yet nobodi

succ after three dai and night should put death

now chanc that old soldier who had been wound battl

the fisherman and hi wife
there wa onc fisherman who live with hi wife pigsti close

the willow wren and the bear
onc summer time the bear and the wolf were walk the forest
and the bear heard bird sing beautifulli that said brother
wolf what bird that sing well that the king bird
said the wolf befor whom must bow down realiti the bird wa
the willow wren that the case said the bear should veri
much like see hi royal palac come take thither that not
done quit you seem think said the wolf you must wait until
the queen come soon afterward the queen arriv with some food
her beak and the lord king came too and thei began fe their young
on the bear would have like onc but the wolf held him
back the sleev and said you must wait until the lord and ladi
queen have gone awai again thei took stock the hole where the
nest lai and trot awai the bear howev could not rest until
had seen the royal palac and when short time had pass went
again the king and queen had just flown out peep and saw
five six young on ly there that the royal palac cri
the bear wretch palac and you ar not king children you
ar disreput children when the young ween heard that thei were
frightfulli angri and scream that ar not our parent ar
honest peopl bear you will have pai for that
the bear and the wolf grew uneasi and turn back and went into their
hole the young willow ween howev continu cry and scream and
when their parent again brought food thei said will not much
touch on fly leg not were dy hunger until you have
settl whether ar respect children not the bear ha been
here and ha insult then the old king said easi shall
punish and onc flew with the queen the bear cave and
call old growler why have you insult children you shall
suffer for will punish you bloodi war thu war wa
announc the bear and all four foot anim were summon take
part oxen ass cow deer and everi other anim the earth
contain and the willow wren summon everyth which flew the
air not onli bird larg and small but midg and hornet bee and
fli had come
when the time came for the war begin the willow wren sent out spi
discov who wa the enemi command chief the gnat who wa
the most crafti flew into the forest where the enemi wa assembl
and hid herself beneath leaf the tree where the password wa
announc there stood the bear and call the fox befor him
and said fox you ar the most cun all anim you shall
gener and lead good said the fox but what signal shall
agre upon on knew that the fox said have fine long
bushi tail which almost look like plume red feather when lift
tail quit high all go well and you must charg but
let hang down run awai fast you can when the gnat had heard
that she flew awai again and reveal everyth down the minutest
detail the willow wren when dai broke and the battl wa begin
all the four foot anim came run with such nois that the
earth trembl the willow wren with hi armi also came fly through
the air with such hum and whir and swarm that everi on
wa uneasi and afraid and both side thei advanc against each
other but the willow wren sent down the hornet with order settl
beneath the fox tail and sting with all hi might when the fox felt
the first string start that lift on leg from pain but
bore and still kept hi tail high the air the second
sting wa forc put down for moment the third could
hold out longer scream and put hi tail between hi leg when
the anim saw that thei thought all wa lost and began flee each
into hi hole and the bird had won the battl
then the king and queen flew home their children and cri
children rejoic eat and drink your heart content have won
the battl but the young ween said will not eat yet the bear
must come the nest and beg for pardon and sai that ar honour
children befor will that then the willow wren flew the
bear hole and cri growler you ar come the nest
children and beg their pardon els everi rib your bodi shall
broken the bear crept thither the greatest fear and beg
their pardon and now last the young bren were satisfi and sat
down togeth and at and drank and made merri till quit late into the
night
the frog princ
on fine even young princess put her bonnet and clog and went
out take walk herself wood and when she came cool
spring water that rose the midst she sat herself down
rest while now she had golden ball her hand which wa her
favourit playth and she wa alwai toss into the air and
catch again fell after time she threw high that
she miss catch fell and the ball bound awai and roll
along upon the ground till last fell down into the spring the
princess look into the spring after her ball but wa veri deep
deep that she could not see the bottom then she began bewail
her loss and said ala could onli get ball again would
give all fine cloth and jewel and everyth that have the
world
whilst she wa speak frog put it head out the water and said
princess why you weep bitterli ala said she what can you
for you nasti frog golden ball ha fallen into the spring
the frog said want not your pearl and jewel and fine cloth
but you will love and let live with you and eat from off
your golden plate and sleep upon your bed will bring you your ball
again what nonsens thought the princess thi silli frog
talk can never even get out the spring visit though
mai abl get ball for and therefor will tell him
shall have what ask she said the frog well you will
bring ball will all you ask then the frog put hi head
down and dive deep under the water and after littl while came
again with the ball hi mouth and threw the edg the
spring soon the young princess saw her ball she ran pick
and she wa overjoi have her hand again that she
never thought the frog but ran home with fast she could
the frog call after her stai princess and take with you you
said but she did not stop hear word
the next dai just the princess had sat down dinner she heard
strang nois tap tap plash plash someth wa come the
marbl staircas and soon afterward there wa gentl knock the
door and littl voic cri out and said
open the door princess dear
open the door thy true love here
and mind the word that thou and said
the fountain cool the greenwood shade
then the princess ran the door and open and there she saw
the frog whom she had quit forgotten thi sight she wa sadli
frighten and shut the door fast she could came back her
seat the king her father see that someth had frighten her
ask her what wa the matter there nasti frog said she
the door that lift ball for out the spring thi morn
told him that should live with here think that could never
get out the spring but there the door and want come
while she wa speak the frog knock again the door and said
open the door princess dear
open the door thy true love here
and mind the word that thou and said
the fountain cool the greenhood shade
then the king said the young princess you have given your word
you must keep and let him she did and the frog hop
into the room and then straight tap tap plash plash from the
bottom the room the top till came close the tabl where
the princess sat prai lift upon chair said the princess
and let sit next you soon she had done thi the frog
said put your plate nearer that mai eat out thi
she did and when had eaten much could said now
tire carri upstair and put into your bed and the princess
though veri unwil took him her hand and put him upon the
pillow her own bed where slept all night long soon wa
light jump hop downstair and went out the hous
now then thought the princess last gone and shall
troubl with him more
but she wa mistaken for when night came again she heard the same
tap the door and the frog came onc more and said
open the door princess dear
open the door thy true love here
and mind the word that thou and said
the fountain cool the greenwood shade
and when the princess open the door the frog came and slept upon
her pillow befor till the morn broke and the third night did
the same but when the princess awok the follow morn she wa
astonish see instead the frog handsom princ gaze her
with the most beauti ey she had ever seen and stand the head
her bed
told her that had been enchant spite fairi who had
chang him into frog and that had been fate abid till
some princess should take him out the spring and let him eat from
her plate and sleep upon her bed for three night you said the
princ have broken hi cruel charm and now have noth wish for
but that you should with into father kingdom where will
marri you and love you long you live
the young princess you mai sure wa not long saye ye
all thi and thei spoke gai coach drove with eight beauti
hors deck with plume feather and golden har and behind
the coach rode the princ servant faith heinrich who had bewail
the misfortun hi dear master dure hi enchant long and
bitterli that hi heart had well nigh burst
thei then took leav the king and got into the coach with eight
hors and all set out full joi and merrim for the princ

mani year
cat and mous partnership
certain cat had made the acquaint mous and had said much
her about the great love and friendship she felt for her that
length the mous agr that thei should live and keep hous togeth
but must make provis for winter els shall suffer
from hunger said the cat and you littl mous cannot ventur
everywher you will caught trap some dai the good advic
wa follow and pot fat wa bought but thei did not know where
put length after much consider the cat said know
place where will better store than the church for on
dare take anyth awai from there will set beneath the altar
and not touch until ar realli ne the pot wa
place safeti but wa not long befor the cat had great
yearn for and said the mous want tell you someth
littl mous cousin ha brought littl son into the world and ha
ask godmoth white with brown spot and hold
him over the font the christen let out todai and you look
after the hous yourself ye ye answer the mous all
mean and you get anyth veri good eat think
should like drop sweet red christen wine myself all thi
howev wa untru the cat had cousin and had not been ask
godmoth she went straight the church stole the pot fat
began lick and lick the top the fat off then she took
walk upon the roof the town look out for opportun and then
stretch herself the sun and lick her lip whenev she thought
the pot fat and not until wa even did she return home
well here you ar again said the mous doubt you have had
merri dai all went off well answer the cat what name did thei
give the child top off said the cat quit coolli top off cri
the mous that veri odd and uncommon name usual on
your famili what doe that matter said the cat wors
than crumb stealer your godchildren ar call
befor long the cat wa seiz anoth fit yearn she said
the mous you must favour and onc more manag the hous for
dai alon again ask godmoth and the child ha
white ring round it neck cannot refus the good mous consent
but the cat crept behind the town wall the church and devour
half the pot fat noth ever seem good what on keep
oneself said she and wa quit satisfi with her dai work when
she went home the mous inquir and what wa the child christen
half done answer the cat half done what ar you saye
never heard the name life wager anyth not the
calendar
the cat mouth soon began water for some more lick all good
thing three said she ask stand godmoth again
the child quit black onli ha white paw but with that
except ha not singl white hair it whole bodi thi onli
happen onc everi few year you will let won you top off
half done answer the mous thei ar such odd name thei make
veri thought you sit home said the cat your dark grei
fur coat and long tail and ar fill with fanci that becaus
you not out the daytim dure the cat absenc the mous
clean the hous and put order but the greedi cat entir
empti the pot fat when everyth eaten on ha some
peac said she herself and well fill and fat she did not return
home till night the mous onc ask what name had been given
the third child will not pleas you more than the other said the
cat call all gone all gone cri the mous that the
most suspici name all have never seen print all gone
what can that mean and she shook her head curl herself and lai
down sleep
from thi time forth on invit the cat godmoth but
when the winter had come and there wa longer anyth found
outsid the mous thought their provis and said come cat
will our pot fat which have store for ourselv
shall enjoi that ye answer the cat you will enjoi much
you would enjoi stick that dainti tongu your out the
window thei set out their wai but when thei arriv the pot
fat certainli wa still it place but wa empti ala said the
mous now see what ha happen now come light you ar true
friend you have devour all when you were stand godmoth first
top off then half done then will you hold your tongu cri the

the goos girl
the king great land di and left hi queen take care their
onli child thi child wa daughter who wa veri beauti and her
mother love her dearli and wa veri kind her and there wa good
fairi too who wa fond the princess and help her mother watch
over her when she grew she wa betroth princ who live
great wai off and the time drew near for her marri she
got readi set off her journei hi countri then the queen her
mother pack great mani costli thing jewel and gold and
silver trinket fine dress and short everyth that becam

her into the bridegroom hand and each had hors for the journei
now the princess hors wa the fairi gift and wa call balada
and could speak
when the time came for them set out the fairi went into her
bed chamber and took littl knife and cut off lock her hair
and gave the princess and said take care dear child for
charm that mai us you the road then thei all took
sorrow leav the princess and she put the lock hair into
her bosom got upon her hors and set off her journei her
bridegroom kingdom
on dai thei were ride along brook the princess began
feel veri thirsti and she said her maid prai get down and fetch
some water golden cup out yonder brook for want
drink nai said the maid you ar thirsti get off yourself and
stoop down the water and drink shall not your wait maid ani
longer then she wa thirsti that she got down and knelt over the
littl brook and drank for she wa frighten and dare not bring out
her golden cup and she wept and said ala what will becom
and the lock answer her and said
ala ala thy mother knew
sadli sadli would she rue
but the princess wa veri gentl and meek she said noth her
maid ill behaviour but got upon her hors again
then all rode farther their journei till the dai grew warm and
the sun scorch that the bride began feel veri thirsti again
and last when thei came river she forgot her maid rude
speech and said prai get down and fetch some water drink
golden cup but the maid answer her and even spoke more haughtili
than befor drink you will but shall not your wait maid
then the princess wa thirsti that she got off her hors and lai
down and held her head over the run stream and cri and said
what will becom and the lock hair answer her again
ala ala thy mother knew
sadli sadli would she rue
and she lean down drink the lock hair fell from her bosom

she had lost the hair when the bride had done drink and would

maid shabbi on
last thei drew near the end their journei thi treacher

happen but balada saw all and mark well

the adventur chanticl and wartlet
how thei went the mountain eat nut
the nut ar quit ripe now said chanticl hi wife portlet
suppos togeth the mountain and eat mani can
befor the squirrel take them all awai with all heart said
wartlet let and make holidai togeth
thei went the mountain and wa love dai thei staye
there till the even now whether wa that thei had eaten mani
nut that thei could not walk whether thei were lazi and would not
not know howev thei took into their head that did not
becom them home foot chanticl began build littl
carriag nutshel and when wa finish tartlet jump into
and sat down and bid chanticl har himself and draw her
home that good joke said chanticl that will never
had rather half walk home sit the box and coachman
you like but not draw while thi wa pass duck came
quack and cri out you thiev vagabond what busi have
you ground give you well for your insol and upon
that she fell upon chanticl most lustili but chanticl wa
coward and return the duck blow with hi sharp spur fierc
that she soon began cry out for merci which wa onli grant her
upon condit that she would draw the carriag home for them thi she
agr and chanticl got upon the box and drove cry now
duck get fast you can and awai thei went pretti good
pace
after thei had travel along littl wai thei met needl and pin
walk togeth along the road and the needl cri out stop stop
and said wa dark that thei could hardli find their wai and such
dirti walk thei could not get all told them that and hi
friend the pin had been public hous few mile off and had sat
drink till thei had forgotten how late wa beg therefor
that the travel would kind give them lift their
carriag chanticl observ that thei were but thin fellow and not
like take much room told them thei might ride but made them
promis not dirti the wheel the carriag get nor
tread tartlet toe
late night thei arriv inn and wa bad travel
the dark and the duck seem much tire and waddl about good
deal from on side the other thei made their mind fix their
quarter there but the landlord first wa unwil and said hi
hous wa full think thei might not veri respect compani
howev thei spoke civilli him and gave him the egg which tartlet
had laid the wai and said thei would give him the duck who wa
the habit laye on everi dai last let them come and
thei bespok handsom supper and spent the even veri jollili
earli the morn befor wa quit light and when nobodi wa
stir the inn chanticl awaken hi wife and fetch the
egg thei peck hole at and threw the shell into the
fireplac thei then went the pin and needl who were fast asleep
and seiz them the head stuck on into the landlord easi chair
and the other into hi handkerchief and have done thi thei crept
awai softli possibl howev the duck who slept the open
air the yard heard them come and jump into the brook which ran
close the inn soon swam out their reach
hour two afterward the landlord got and took hi handkerchief
wipe hi face but the pin ran into him and prick him then
walk into the kitchen light hi pipe the fire but when
stir the eggshel flew into hi ey and almost blind him
bless said all the world seem have design against
head thi morn and saye threw himself sulkili into hi
easi chair but dear the needl ran into him and thi time the
pain wa not hi head now flew into veri great passion and
suspect the compani who had come the night befor went look
after them but thei were all off swore that never again
would take such troop vagabond who at great deal paid
reckon and gave him noth for hi troubl but their apish trick
how chanticl and martlet went visit korb

rapunzel
there were onc man and woman who had long vain wish for
child length the woman hope that god wa about grant her desir
these peopl had littl window the back their hous from which
splendid garden could seen which wa full the most beauti
flower and herb wa howev surround high wall and
on dare into becaus belong enchantress who had
great power and wa dread all the world on dai the woman wa

ask what ail you dear wife she repli can eat
some the rampion which the garden behind our hous shall

answer let merci take the place justic onli made
mind out necess wife saw your rampion from the
window and felt such long for that she would have di she

condit you must give the child which your wife will bring into

the valiant littl tailor

hansel and gretel
hard great forest dwelt poor wood cutter with hi wife and hi
two children the boi wa call hans and the girl gretel had
littl bite and break and onc when great dearth fell the
land could longer procur even daili bread now when thought
over thi night hi bed and toss about hi anxieti
groan and said hi wife what becom how ar
fe our poor children when longer have anyth even for
ourselv tell you what husband answer the woman earli
tomorrow morn will take the children out into the forest where
the thickest there will light fire for them and give each
them on more piec bread and then will our work and
leav them alon thei will not find the wai home again and shall
rid them wife said the man will not that how can
bear leav children alon the forest the wild anim would
soon come and tear them piec you fool said she then
must all four die hunger you mai well plane the plank for our
coffin and she left him peac until consent but feel veri
sorri for the poor children all the same said the man
the two children had also not been abl sleep for hunger and had
heard what their stepmoth had said their father gretel wept

mother hole
onc upon time there wa widow who had two daughter on them

the littl peasant

but stipul that clever elsin should realli smart said
the father she ha plenti good sens and the mother said
she can see the wind come the street and hear the fli cough
well said hand she not realli smart won have her when
thei were sit dinner and had eaten the mother said elsin
into the cellar and fetch some beer then clever elsin took the pitcher
from the wall went into the cellar and tap the lid briskli she
went that the time might not appear long when she wa below she
fetch herself chair and set befor the barrel that she had
ne stoop and did not hurt her back herself ani unexpect
injuri then she place the can befor her and turn the tap and
while the beer wa run she would not let her ey idl but
look the wall and after much peer here and there saw
pick ax exactli abov her which the mason had accident left
there
then clever elsin began weep and said get hand and have
child and grow big and send him into the cellar here draw
beer then the pick ax will fall hi head and kill him then she
sat and wept and scream with all the strength her bodi over the
misfortun which lai befor her those upstair wait for the drink
but clever elsin still did not come then the woman said the servant
just down into the cellar and see where elsin the maid went and
found her sit front the barrel scream loudli elsin why
you weep ask the maid she answer have not reason
weep get hand and have child and grow big and ha
draw beer here the pick ax will perhap fall hi head and kill
him then said the maid what clever elsin have and sat down

elsin and the girl both weep togeth then ask why ar you
weep said elsin have not reason weep get hand
and have child and grow big and ha draw beer here the
pick ax will fall hi head and kill him then said the boi what
clever elsin have and sat down her and likewis began
howl loudli upstair thei wait for the boi but still did not
return the man said the woman just down into the cellar and see
where elsin the woman went down and found all three the midst

her also that her futur child wa kill the pick ax when
grew big and had draw beer and the pick ax fell down then said the
mother likewis what clever elsin have and sat down and wept

that elsin child wa the caus and the elsin might perhap bring on
into the world some dai and that might kill the pick ax
should happen sit beneath drawe beer just the veri
time when fell down cri what clever elsin and sat
down and likewis wept with them the bridegroom staye upstair alon
for long time then on would come back thought thei must
wait for below too must there and see what thei ar about
when got down the five them were sit scream and lament

then ask dear hand said elsin marri each other
and have child and big and perhap send him here draw

weep come said hand more understand than that not need

after hand had had her some time said wife go out work
and earn some monei for into the field and cut the corn that
mai have some bread ye dear hand will that after hand had

more said what shall shall cut first shall sleep first

hand had been home for long time but elsin did not come then said
what clever elsin have she industri that she doe not

hand went out see what she had cut but noth wa cut and she

the miser the bush
farmer had faith and dilig servant who had work hard for
him three year without have been paid ani wage last came
into the man head that would not thu without pai ani longer
went hi master and said have work hard for you long

the white snake
long time ago there live king who wa fame for hi wisdom through
all the land noth wa hidden from him and seem new
the most secret thing wa brought him through the air but had
strang custom everi dai after dinner when the tabl wa clear
and on els wa present trusti servant had bring him on more

the wolf and the seven littl kid
there wa onc upon time old goat who had seven littl kid and
love them with all the love mother for her children on dai she
want into the forest and fetch some food she call all

the clock case wa the onli on did not find when the wolf had

tree the green meadow outsid and began sleep soon afterward
the old goat came home again from the forest what sight she saw
there the hous door stood wide open the tabl chair and bench
were thrown down the wash bowl lai broken piec and the quilt
and pillow were pull off the bed she sought her children but thei
were nowher found she call them on after anoth name but
on answer last when she came the youngest soft voic
cri dear mother the clock case she took the kid out and
told her that the wolf had come and had eaten all the other then
you mai imagin how she wept over her poor children
length her grief she went out and the youngest kid ran with her
when thei came the meadow there lai the wolf the tree and snore
loud that the branch shook she look him everi side and
saw that someth wa move and struggl hi gorg belli

swallow down for hi supper can still aliv then the kid had

the three languag

the youth wander and after some time came fortress where

the fox and the cat
happen that the cat met the fox forest and she thought
herself clever and full experi and much esteem the
world she spoke him friendli wai good dai dear fox
how ar you how all with you how ar you get these hard
time the fox full all kind arrog look the cat from

ani answer not last said you wretch beard cleaner you

you the cheek ask how get what have you learnt how

said the fox master hundr art and have into the bargain

abl climb like you would not have lost your life
the four clever brother
dear children said poor man hi four son have noth

learn some craft anoth and see how you can get the

each brother went hi wai and the eldest wa hasten man

go try luck the world and should like begin learn

will teach you becom the cunningest thief that ever wa

the third brother met huntsman who took him with him and taught him

well done son said the old man you have made good us your

then awai thei hasten with her full joi their boat toward the

when thei had brought home the princess her father there wa great

lili and the lion
merchant who had three daughter wa onc set out upon
journei but befor went ask each daughter what gift should
bring back for her the eldest wish for pearl the second for jewel
but the third who wa call lili said dear father bring
rose now wa easi task find rose for wa the middl
winter yet she wa hi prettiest daughter and wa veri fond
flower her father said would try what could kiss all
three and bid them goodby
and when the time came for him home had bought pearl and
jewel for the two eldest but had sought everywher vain for the
rose and when went into ani garden and ask for such thing the
peopl laugh him and ask him whether thought rose grew
snow thi griev him veri much for lili wa hi dearest child and
wa journei home think what should bring her came
fine castl and around the castl wa garden on half which
seem summer time and the other half winter on side the
finest flower were full bloom and the other everyth look
dreari and buri the snow lucki hit said call
hi servant and told him beauti bed rose that wa
there and bring him awai on the finest flower
thi done thei were ride awai well pleas when sprang fierc
lion and roar out whoever ha stolen rose shall eaten
aliv then the man said knew not that the garden belong you
can noth save life said the lion noth unless you
undertak give whatev meet you your return home you
agre thi will give you your life and the rose too for your
daughter but the man wa unwil and said mai
youngest daughter who love most and alwai run meet when
home then the servant wa greatli frighten and said mai
perhap onli cat dog and last the man yield with
heavi heart and took the rose and said would give the lion whatev
should meet him first hi return
and came near home wa lili hi youngest and dearest
daughter that met him she came run and kiss him and welcom
him home and when she saw that had brought her the rose she wa
still more glad but her father began veri sorrow and weep
saye ala dearest child have bought thi flower high

had happen and said she should not let what would happen

must kept will the lion and sooth him perhap will let
come safe home again
the next morn she ask the wai she wa and took leav her

enchant princ dai and all hi court were lion but the
even thei took their right form again and when lili came the

princ wa onli seen soon even came and then held hi
court but everi morn left hi bride and went awai himself
she knew not whither till the night came again

staye till the feast wa over and then went back the wood

the wed she said the princ will not alon thi
time you must with but would not and said that would
veri hazard thing for the least rai the torch light should
fall upon him hi enchant would becom still wors for should
chang into dove and forc wander about the world for seven

light should fall upon him last thei set out togeth and
took with them their littl child and she chose larg hall with thick
wall for him sit while the wed torch were light but
unluckili on saw that there wa crack the door then the

and pass with the torch befor the hall veri small rai light
fell upon the princ moment disappear and when hi wife came
and look for him she found onli white dove and said her
seven year must fly and down over the face the earth but
everi now and then will let fall white feather that will show you
the wai go follow and last you mai overtak and set
free
thi said flew out the door and poor lili follow and everi
now and then white feather fell and showe her the wai she wa
journei thu she went rove through the wide world and look
neither the right hand nor the left nor took ani rest for seven
year then she began glad and thought herself that the time
wa fast come when all her troubl should end yet repos wa still
far off for on dai she wa travel she miss the white
feather and when she lift her ey she could nowher see the dove
now thought she herself aid man can us
she went the sun and said thou shiniest everywher the hill
top and the vallei depth hast thou anywher seen white dove
said the sun have not seen but will give thee
casket open when thy hour ne come
she thank the sun and went her wai till eventid and when
the moon aros she cri unto and said thou shiniest through the
night over field and grove hast thou nowher seen white dove
said the moon cannot help thee but will give thee
egg break when ne come
then she thank the moon and went till the night wind blew and she
rais her voic and said thou slowest through everi tree
and under everi leaf hast thou not seen white dove said the
night wind but will ask three other wind perhap thei have seen
then the east wind and the west wind came and said thei too had
not seen but the south wind said have seen the white dove
ha fled the red sea and chang onc more into lion for the
seven year ar pass awai and there fight with dragon
and the dragon enchant princess who seek separ him from
you then the night wind said will give thee counsel the
red sea the right shore stand mani rod count them and when thou
comet the eleventh break off and smite the dragon with and
the lion will have the victori and both them will appear you
their own form then look round and thou will see griffin wing
like bird sit the red sea jump hi back with thy belov
on quickli possibl and will carri you over the water
your home will also give thee thi nut continu the night wind
when you ar half wai over throw down and out the water will
immedi spring high nut tree which the griffin will abl
rest otherwis would not have the strength bear you the whole
wai therefor thou dost forget throw down the nut will let
you both fall into the sea
our poor wander went forth and found all the night wind had
said and she pluck the eleventh rod and smote the dragon and the
lion forthwith becam princ and the dragon princess again but
sooner wa the princess releas from the spell than she seiz

carri the princ awai with her
thu the unhappi travel wa again forsaken and forlorn but she

long long wai till length she came the castl whither the

that within lai dress dazzl the sun itself she put

the dress pleas the bride much that she ask whether wa

the princess ask what she meant and she said let speak with the

last the princess agr but she told her chamberlain give the
princ sleep draught that might not hear see her when
even came and the princ had fallen asleep she wa led into

night wind seek thee and last have help thee overcom
the dragon will thou then forget quit but the princ all the time
slept soundli that her voic onli pass over him and seem like
the whistl the wind among the fir tree
then poor lili wa led awai and forc give the golden dress and
when she saw that there wa help for her she went out into meadow

then the princess thought betrai her befor and agr

the fox and the hors
farmer had hors that had been excel faith servant
him but wa now grown too old work the farmer would give him
noth more eat and said want you longer take yourself
off out stabl shall not take you back again until you ar
stronger than lion then open the door and turn him adrift

down your head and look lone and woe begon repli the
hors justic and avaric never dwell on hous master ha

the blue light
there wa onc upon time soldier who for mani year had serv the
king faithfulli but when the war came end could serv longer
becaus the mani wound which had receiv the king said him
you mai return your home ne you longer and you will not

servic for them then the soldier did not know how earn live

even enter forest when dark came saw light

the poor soldier fell without injuri the moist ground and the blue

the raven

the golden goos

doctor nowal
there wa onc upon time poor peasant call crab who drove with
two oxen load wood the town and sold doctor for two
taler when the monei wa be count out him happen that
the doctor wa sit tabl and when the peasant saw how well
at and drank hi heart desir what saw and would willingli

the seven ravel
there wa onc man who had seven son and last all on daughter

the salad
merri young huntsman wa onc go briskli along through wood
there came littl old woman and said him good dai good dai
you seem merri enough but hungri and thirsti prai give
someth eat the huntsman took piti her and put hi hand

she took hold him and said listen friend what go

sit cloak shoot into the midst them and on will fall down
dead the cloak will fall too take wish cloak and when
you wear you will find yourself ani place where you mai wish
cut open the dead bird take out it heart and keep and you will
find piec gold under your pillow everi morn when you rise
the bird heart that will bring you thi good luck
the huntsman thank her and thought himself all thi doe
happen will fine thing for when had gone hundr
step heard scream and chirp the branch over him
and look and saw flock bird pull cloak with their bill
and feet scream fight and tug each other
each wish have himself well said the huntsman thi
